# Abstract

This gazetteer compiles coordinates, historical names and benchmark
population estimates for 1159 settlements and archaeological sites
across the Wild Fields and Budjak (~238k km²) between the Lower Danube
and the Seversky Donets from 10000BC to 1400AD. Prehistoric entries
cover Ice Age and Mesolithic river camps (Anetovka II, Mirnoe and the
Vasylivka rapids cemeteries), Copper Age Bolgrad-Aldeni and Usatovo
villages, the Yamnaya stone fort at Mikhailovka and Late Bronze Age
Sabatinovka farming villages and Bakhmut copper mines (Kartamysh).
Classical and Late Antique entries track the Milesian Greek ports of
Pontic Olbia (12k - 15k in 500BC), Tyras (6k), Nikonion and Berezan, the
1,2k ha Scythian ironworking town of Kamenskoye Gorodishche (6k), the 16
Late Scythian stone forts on the Lower Dnieper and the dense village
network of the Chernyakhov culture up to 375AD. Medieval entries follow
the *Dridu* villages of Budjak, the Khazar-era Saltovo-Mayaki forts on
the Seversky Donets (led by Sidorovo at 5k in 900AD), the Kievan Rus
river ports of Oleshye (3,5k in 1200AD), Protolche and Igren-Samar, the
Polovtsian winter towns of Sharukhan, Sugrov and Balin, and the
fourteenth-century Golden Horde, Genoese, Moldavian and Lithuanian ports
of Aq Qerman (15k - 16k in 1300AD), Kiliia (6,5k - 8k), Velyki Kuchugury
(5,5k - 6k), Dashov and Khadzhibey.

# Methodology

The 1159 settlements and archaeological sites of the Wild Fields and
Budjak (~238k km²) recorded between 10000BC and 1400AD are listed below
across its five regions: Budjak (I), the north-western Pontic steppe
(II), the right-bank Bug-Dnieper and Ingul-Ingulets uplands (III), the
left-bank Lower Dnieper, Veliky Lug and northern Taurida steppe (IV) and
the Middle Seversky Donets and Azov basin (V).

Each entry gives the settlement name, historical or alternative names,
coordinates, dates of occupation and population estimates. Figures are
based on site area and house counts for prehistoric camps and Bronze Age
villages, Greek town and rural hinterland surveys, *Dridu* and
*Saltovo-Mayaki* fort enclosures, Kievan Rus chronicle and excavation
evidence and Golden Horde, Genoese, Moldavian and Lithuanian port
records up to 1400AD
[\[1\]](#ref-kryzhitsky1999)[\[2\]](#ref-subbotin2000)[\[3\]](#ref-kravchenko2020)[\[4\]](#ref-dovzhenok1961)[\[5\]](#ref-bocharov2015)[\[6\]](#ref-boguslavskiy2014).

| Period | Sites | Main Settlements & Sites |
|:---|:---|:---|
| I. Prehistoric, Copper Age & Bronze Age (10000BC - 1000BC) | 662 | Anetovka II, Mirnoe \[Budjak\], Vovnigi, Mariupol, Dereivka, Bolhrad-Yalpug \[Budjak\], Usatovo, Mikhailovka, Sabatinovka, Kartamysh |
| II\. Classical Greek Ports, Scythia, Late Scythian & Chernyakhov (700BC - 400AD) | 393 | Tyras \[Budjak\], Orlovka/Aliobrix \[Budjak\], Olbia, Berezan, Nikonion, Kamenskoye, Chertomlyk, Zolota Balka, Krasny Mayak, Bashmachka |
| III\. Early Medieval, Dridu (Budjak), Saltovo-Mayaki, Kievan Rus & Cuman (500AD - 1200AD) | 89 | Voznesenka, Suvorovo-Katlabukh \[Budjak\], Sidorovo, Mayaki-Tsaritsyno, Sukhaya Gomolsha, Oleshye, Protolche, Igren-Samar, Sharukhan |
| IV\. Golden Horde, Genoese, Moldavian-Budjak & Lithuanian Ports (1240AD - 1400AD) | 15 | Aq Qerman / Maurocastro \[Budjak\], Kiliia / Lycostomo \[Budjak\], Smil / Izmail \[Budjak\], Kuchugury, Konski Vody, Khadzhibey, Dashov |
| Total Gazetteer Settlements | 1159 | All five regions of the Wild Fields and Budjak |

Breakdown of the 1159 settlements and sites in the Wild Fields and
Budjak by period of first record, showing the main prehistoric camps,
Classical ports, early medieval forts and Golden Horde trading towns.

# Results

## Prehistoric, Copper Age and Bronze Age Sites

Table lists the 662 prehistoric sites first attested between 10000BC and
1000BC, including Ice Age and Mesolithic hunting camps, the Dnieper
Rapids cemeteries, Bolgrad-Aldeni (Gumelnita) and Usatovo Copper Age
villages, Yamnaya forts and Sabatinovka-Srubnaya farming and
copper-mining settlements

Greek coastal towns (Olbia, Tyras, Nikonion and Berezan), Royal Scythian
centres, Late Scythian Lower Dnieper stone forts and Chernyakhov
villages first occupied between 700BC and 400AD.

## Medieval Settlements and Ports

Table lists the 104 settlements first attested between 500AD and 1400AD,
covering Antae/Penkovka rapids villages, the Voznesenka camp, the
*Dridu* villages of Budjak, the *Saltovo-Mayaki* forts on the Seversky
Donets, the Kievan Rus ports of Oleshye and Protolche, the Polovtsian
winter towns and the Golden Horde, Genoese, Moldavian and Lithuanian
ports of Aq Qerman (Maurocastro), Kiliia (Lycostomo), Smil (Izmail),
Kuchugury, Mamai-Surka, Khadzhibey and Dashov
[\[3\]](#ref-kravchenko2020)[\[4\]](#ref-dovzhenok1961)[\[5\]](#ref-bocharov2015)[\[6\]](#ref-boguslavskiy2014).

Early and late medieval settlements founded between 500AD and 1400AD,
tracking Danubian Bulgarian villages in Budjak, Khazar-era Saltovo forts
on the Donets, Kievan Rus river ports and fourteenth-century Golden
Horde and Italian trading towns.
